There is Your testimony, which gives wisdom to little ones; complete Your praise, my God, out of the mouths of babies and infants. For we know no other books that so destroy pride, that so destroy the enemy and the defender who resists Your reconciling by defending his own sins. I know of no other words so pure, Lord, that so persuade me to confess, and make my neck pliant to Your yoke, and invite me to serve You for nothing. Let me understand them, good Father; grant this to me, who am set under them, since it was for those set under them that You established them.

There are other waters above this firmament, immortal, I believe, and separated from earthly corruption. Let them praise Your name — let them praise You, the people above the heavens, Your angels, who have no need to gaze up at this firmament or to learn Your Word by reading. For they always look on Your face, and there they read, without any syllables in time, what Your eternal will wills. They read, they choose, they love. They are always reading, and what they read never passes away; for by choosing and by loving they read the very unchangeableness of Your counsel. Their book is never closed, their scroll never rolled up, since You Yourself are this to them, and are so eternally; because You have set them above this firmament, which You have firmly settled over the weakness of the people below, where they might gaze up and learn Your mercy, which announces in time You who made times. For Your mercy, Lord, is in the heavens, and Your truth reaches to the clouds. The clouds pass away, but the sky remains. The preachers of Your word pass out of this life into another; but Your Scripture is spread out over the peoples to the end of the world. And yet heaven and earth also shall pass away, but Your words shall not pass away. Because the scroll shall be rolled up, and the grass it was spread over shall pass away with its beauty; but Your Word remains for ever, which now appears to us under the dark image of the clouds, and through the glass of the heavens, and not as it is. Because we too, though we are the beloved of Your Son, it has not yet appeared what we shall be. He looks through the lattice of our flesh, and he spoke tenderly to us, and set us alight, and we ran after his scent. But when he appears, then we shall be like him, for we shall see him as he is. As he is, Lord, will our sight be.

For only You know altogether as You are — You who are unchangeably, and know unchangeably, and will unchangeably. And Your being knows and wills unchangeably; and Your knowing is and wills unchangeably; and Your willing is and knows unchangeably. And it does not seem right in Your eyes that as the unchangeable light knows itself, so it should be known by the thing that is lit and changeable. And so my soul is like a land where there is no water, because just as it cannot light itself by itself, so it cannot satisfy itself by itself. For so is the fountain of life with You, just as in Your light we shall see light.

Who gathered the embittered into one society? For they all have one end, a temporary and earthly happiness, and everything they do is to get it, though they toss up and down with a countless variety of cares. Who but You, Lord, said, Let the waters be gathered together into one place, and let the dry land appear, which thirsts for You? For the sea is Yours, and You made it, and Your hands prepared the dry land. And it is not the bitterness of men's wills but the gathering of the waters that is called sea; for You hold back the wicked desires of men's souls and set their limits, how far they may be allowed to go, so that their waves break against each other; and so You make it a sea, by the order of Your rule over all things.

But the souls that thirst for You and appear before You, separated by other boundaries from the society of the sea, You water with a sweet spring, so that the earth may bring forth her fruit; and at Your command, Lord God, our soul may bud with works of mercy after their kind, loving our neighbour in the relief of his bodily needs, and having seed in itself after its likeness, when out of a feeling of our own weakness we have compassion enough to relieve the needy, helping them as we would want to be helped if we were in the same need. And not only in easy things, like a plant yielding seed, but in the protection of our help, with our best strength, like a tree yielding fruit: that is, doing good by rescuing a man who suffers wrong out of the hand of the powerful, and giving him the shelter of protection by the strength of a just judgement.

So, Lord, so, I beg You, let there spring up, as You do and as You give cheerfulness and ability — let truth spring out of the earth, and righteousness look down from heaven, and let there be lights in the firmament. Let us break our bread for the hungry and bring the homeless poor into our house. Let us clothe the naked, and not despise those of our own flesh. And when these fruits have sprung up out of the earth, see that it is good; and let our temporary light break out; and let us, arriving from this lower fruitfulness of action at the delight of contemplation, and taking hold of the Word of life above, appear like lights in the world, holding fast to the firmament of Your Scripture. For there You teach us to divide between things of the mind and things of the senses, as between day and night; or between souls given to things of the mind and souls given to things of the senses. So that now it is not only You in the secret of Your judgement, as before the firmament was made, who divide between the light and the darkness, but Your spiritual children too, set and ranked in the same firmament, now that Your grace is laid open throughout the world, may give light on the earth and divide between the day and the night, and be for signs of the times: that old things have passed away, and all things have become new; that our salvation is nearer than when we first believed; that the night is far gone and the day is at hand; and that You will crown Your year with blessing, sending the labourers of Your goodness into Your harvest, in whose sowing others have laboured, and sending them also into another field whose harvest shall be at the end. So You grant the prayers of the man who asks, and bless the years of the just; but You are the same, and in Your years, which do not run out, You prepare a barn for our passing years.

For by an eternal counsel You bestow heavenly blessings on the earth in their proper seasons. To one is given by the Spirit the word of wisdom, like the greater light, for those who delight in the light of clear truth, as it were to rule the day. To another the word of knowledge by the same Spirit, like the lesser light; to another faith; to another the gift of healing; to another the working of miracles; to another prophecy; to another the discerning of spirits; to another different kinds of tongues. And all these are like stars. For all these the one and the same Spirit works, giving to each man his own as he wills, and making stars appear plainly, for the profit of all. But the word of knowledge, which contains all the sacraments, which vary in their seasons like the moon, and those other notices of gifts which are reckoned up in order like stars — in so far as they fall short of that brightness of wisdom which gladdens the day I spoke of, they are only for the ruling of the night. For they are necessary to the kind of people that even Your most prudent servant could not speak to as spiritual but only as unspiritual: he who speaks wisdom among those who are grown up. But the natural man, like a baby in Christ fed on milk until he is strong enough for solid food and his eye is able to look at the sun, let him not live in a night forsaken by all light, but be content with the light of the moon and the stars. So You speak to us, our all-wise God, in Your book, Your firmament, so that we may make everything out in an admirable contemplation, though as yet in signs and in times and in days and in years.

But first, wash yourselves, be clean; put away evil from your souls and from before my eyes, so that the dry land may appear. Learn to do good, judge for the fatherless, plead for the widow, so that the earth may bring forth green plants for food, and the tree bearing fruit; and come, let us reason together, says the Lord, so that there may be lights in the firmament of heaven and they may shine on the earth.

That rich man asked the good master what he should do to gain eternal life. Let the good master tell him — whom he took for no more than a man, but he is good because he is God — let him tell him that if he wants to enter into life he must keep the commandments; let him put away from himself the bitterness of malice and wickedness; not kill, not commit adultery, not steal, not bear false witness; so that the dry land may appear and bring forth the honouring of father and mother and the love of our neighbour. All these, he says, I have kept. Then where do so many thorns come from, if the earth is fruitful? Go and root up the spreading thickets of greed; sell what you have, and be filled with fruit by giving to the poor, and you shall have treasure in heaven; and follow the Lord if you want to be perfect, joining those among whom he speaks wisdom, who knows what to allot to the day and to the night — so that you too may know it, and so that for you there may be lights in the firmament of heaven, which there will not be unless your heart is there; and that will not be either, unless your treasure is there, as you have heard from the good master. But that barren earth was grieved, and the thorns choked the word.

But you, chosen generation, you weak things of the world, who have left everything to follow the Lord: go after him and confound the mighty; go after him, beautiful feet, and shine in the firmament, so that the heavens may declare his glory, dividing between the light of the mature — though not like the angels — and the darkness of the little ones, who are nevertheless not despised. Shine over the earth; and let the day, lit by the sun, pour out to the day the speech of wisdom; and let the night, shining with the moon, show to the night the word of knowledge. The moon and the stars shine for the night, and the night does not blot them out, since they give it what light it has. For when God said, as it were, Let there be lights in the firmament of heaven, there came suddenly a sound from heaven, like a rushing mighty wind, and there appeared divided tongues as of fire, and one sat on each of them. And lights were made in the firmament of heaven, holding the word of life. Run everywhere, holy fires, beautiful fires; for you are the light of the world, and you are not put under a bucket. He whom you hold fast to is lifted high, and has lifted you high. Run to and fro, and be known to all nations.

Let the sea also conceive and bring forth your works; and let the waters bring forth the moving creature that has life. For you, separating the precious from the worthless, are made the mouth of God, by whom he says, Let the waters bring forth — not the living creature the earth brings forth, but the moving creature that has life, and the birds that fly above the earth. For Your sacraments, God, by the ministry of Your holy ones, have moved among the waves of the world's temptations, to make the Gentiles holy in Your name, in Your baptism. And in the middle of all this many great wonders were worked, like great whales; and the voices of Your messengers flying above the earth, in the open firmament of Your book, which was set over them as the authority they were to fly under, wherever they went. For there is no speech or language where their voice is not heard, since their sound has gone through all the earth and their words to the end of the world, because You, Lord, multiplied them by blessing them.

Am I speaking untruly, or mixing things up and confusing them, and failing to distinguish between the clear knowledge of these things in the firmament of heaven and the material works in the wavy sea and under the firmament of heaven? For of those things whose knowledge is substantial and defined, without any increase by generation — lights of wisdom and knowledge, as it were — even of those the material workings are many and various; and one thing growing out of another, they are multiplied by Your blessing, God, who have refreshed the fastidiousness of mortal senses, so that one thing in the understanding of our mind may be set out and expressed in many ways by the motions of the body. It is the waters that have brought these sacraments forth, but in Your word. The needs of a people estranged from the eternity of Your truth have brought them forth, but in Your gospel; because the waters themselves cast them out, and the diseased bitterness of the waters was the reason they were sent out in Your Word.

Everything You have made is beautiful now; and yet You Yourself, who made it all, are unspeakably more beautiful. And if Adam had not fallen away from You, the brackishness of the sea would never have flowed out of him — that is, the human race, so deeply curious and stormily swelling and restlessly tumbling up and down. And then there would have been no need for Your stewards to work in many waters, in a bodily and perceptible way, at mysterious doings and sayings. For that is what those moving and flying creatures now seem to me to mean: that people initiated and consecrated by bodily sacraments should not get any further unless their soul had a spiritual life, and unless after the word of admission it looked forward to completion.

And by this, in Your Word, it is not the deepness of the sea but the earth separated from the bitterness of the waters that brings forth, not the moving creature that has life but the living soul. For now it no longer needs baptism, as the heathen do and as it did itself when it was covered with the waters — for there is no other entrance into the kingdom of heaven, since You have appointed that this should be the entrance. Nor does it look for marvellous miracles to produce belief; for it is not the kind that will not believe unless it sees signs and wonders, now that the faithful earth has been separated from the waters that were bitter with unbelief, and tongues are a sign not for those who believe but for those who do not. So the earth You have founded on the waters does not need that flying kind which at Your word the waters brought forth. Send Your word into it by Your messengers; for we speak of their working, and yet it is You who work in them, so that they may work out a living soul in it. The earth brings it forth, because the earth is the reason they work this in the soul, as the sea was the reason they worked on the moving creatures that have life and the birds that fly under the firmament of heaven, which the earth does not need — although it feeds on that fish taken out of the deep, at that table You have prepared in the presence of those who believe. For that was why he was taken out of the deep: so that he might feed the dry land. And the bird, though bred in the sea, is nevertheless multiplied on the earth. For it was man's unbelief that made the first preachings of the evangelists necessary; and yet the faithful too are encouraged and blessed by them in many ways, from day to day. But the living soul takes its beginning from the earth: for it profits only those who are already among the faithful to hold themselves back from the love of this world, so that their soul may live for You — the soul that was dead while it lived in pleasures. In death-bringing pleasures, Lord; for You, Lord, are the life-giving delight of the pure heart.

Now let Your ministers work on the earth — not as on the waters of unbelief, by preaching and speaking through miracles and sacraments and mysterious words, in which ignorance, the mother of wonder, might be intent on them out of reverence for those secret signs. For that is the entrance into faith for the sons of Adam who have forgotten You, while they hide themselves from Your face and become a dark deep. But let Your ministers now work as on dry land, separated from the whirlpools of the great deep; and let them be a pattern to the faithful by living in front of them and stirring them up to imitation. For that is how men hear, so as not only to hear but to do. Seek the Lord, and your soul shall live, so that the earth may bring forth the living soul. Do not be conformed to the world. Hold yourselves back from it: the soul lives by avoiding what it dies by wanting. Hold yourselves back from the ungoverned wildness of pride, the sluggish self-indulgence of luxury, and the falsely named knowledge — so that the wild beasts may be tamed, the cattle broken to the yoke, the serpents made harmless. For these are the movements of our mind, under an allegory: the haughtiness of pride, the pleasure of lust and the poison of curiosity are the movements of a dead soul. For a soul does not die so as to lose all movement; it dies by abandoning the fountain of life, and so is taken up by this passing world and conformed to it.

But Your word, God, is the fountain of eternal life and does not pass away. So this departure of the soul is held back by Your word, when it is said to us, Do not be conformed to this world — so that the earth may bring forth a living soul in the fountain of life: that is, a soul made self-controlled in Your Word, by Your evangelists, by following the followers of Your Christ. For this is after his kind, because a man usually imitates his friend. Be as I am, he says, for I am as you are. So in this living soul there will be good beasts, in gentleness of action — for You have commanded, Go about your business gently, and so you will be loved by everyone. And good cattle, which neither have too much if they eat nor go short if they do not. And good serpents, not dangerous enough to do harm but wise enough to be careful, and searching into this temporal nature only so far as is enough for eternity to be clearly seen and understood by the things that are made. For these creatures are obedient to reason when, being held back from fatally getting the better of us, they live and are good.

For look, Lord our God, our creator, when our affections have been held back from the love of the world, by which we died through living badly, and have begun to be a living soul through living well; and the word You spoke through Your apostle is made good in us, Do not be conformed to this world — then that follows which You immediately added: But be transformed by the renewing of your mind. Not now after your kind, as though following a neighbour who went before you, and not living by the example of some better man. For You did not say, "Let man be made after his kind", but, Let us make man after our own image and likeness — so that we might test what Your will is. For it was to this end that that steward of Yours, who fathered children by the gospel, said this, so that he should not have them for ever as babies whom he would have to feed on milk and cherish like a nurse: Be transformed, he says, by the renewing of your mind, so that you may test what is that good and acceptable and perfect will of God. And so You do not say, "Let man be made", but Let us make man. And You did not say, "according to his kind", but after our image and likeness. For a man renewed in his mind, and looking at and understanding Your truth, does not need a man as his director, so as to follow after his kind; by Your direction he tests what is that good and acceptable and perfect will of Yours. Indeed You teach him, now that he is capable of it, to make out the Trinity in the unity and the unity in the Trinity. And so to the words said in the plural, Let us make man, there is added in the singular, And God made man; and to the words said in the plural, After our likeness, there is added in the singular, After the image of God. So a man is renewed in the knowledge of God, after the image of him who created him; and being made spiritual, he judges all things — all things that are to be judged — while he himself is judged by nobody.

And that he judges all things answers to his having dominion over the fish of the sea and over the birds of the air, and over all the cattle and the wild beasts, and over all the earth, and over every creeping thing that creeps on the earth. He does this by the understanding of his mind, by which he perceives the things of the Spirit of God; whereas otherwise, a man set in a position of honour had no understanding, and is compared to the brute beasts and has become like them. So in Your church, our God, according to the grace You have given it — for we are Your workmanship created for good works — it is not only those who are set over others spiritually but those who are spiritually subject to those set over them. For it was in this way that You made man male and female, in Your spiritual grace, where, as regards the sex of the body, there is neither male nor female, because there is neither Jew nor Greek, neither slave nor free. Spiritual people, whether they are set over others or obey, judge spiritually. They do not judge the spiritual knowledge that shines in the firmament, since they ought not to judge so supreme an authority; and they may not judge Your book itself, even where something in it does not shine clearly, since we submit our understanding to it and hold it certain that even what is closed to our sight is rightly and truly said. For a man, though now spiritual and renewed in the knowledge of God after the image of him who created him, ought to be a doer of the law and not a judge of it. Nor does he judge that distinction between spiritual and unspiritual men which is known to Your eyes, our God, and which they have not yet revealed to us by their works, so that we might know them by their fruits; but You, Lord, already know them, and have divided and called them in secret before ever the firmament was made. Nor does he, spiritual though he is, judge the restless people of this world; for what has he to do with judging those who are outside, when he does not know which of them will later come into the sweetness of Your grace, and which will stay in the perpetual bitterness of irreligion?

So man, whom You made after Your own image, did not receive dominion over the lights of heaven, or over that hidden heaven itself, or over the day and the night which You named before the foundation of the sky, or over the gathering of the waters which is the sea. He received dominion over the fish of the sea and the birds of the air, and over all the cattle, and over all the earth, and over all the creeping things that creep on the earth. For he judges and approves what he finds right and rejects what he finds wrong: whether in the celebration of the sacraments by which those are initiated whom Your mercy searches out in many waters; or in that in which the Fish is set out, taken out of the deep, which the devout earth feeds on; or in the expressions and signs of words, subject to the authority of Your book — signs which come out of the mouth and sound out, flying as it were under the firmament, in interpreting, expounding, discussing, arguing, blessing or praying to You, so that the people may answer, Amen. And the reason all these words have to be pronounced aloud is the deep of this world and the blindness of the flesh, which cannot see thoughts; so that there is a need to speak aloud into the ears. So that although flying birds are multiplied on the earth, they take their beginning from the waters. The spiritual man also judges by approving what is right and rejecting what he finds wrong in the works and lives of the faithful: their alms, which are like the earth bringing forth fruit; and the living soul, living by the taming of the affections, in chastity, in fasting, in holy meditation; and those things that are perceived by the senses of the body. Over all these he is now said to judge, in which he also has the power of correction.

But what is this, and what kind of mystery is it? You bless mankind, Lord, so that they may increase and multiply and fill the earth. Are You not giving us a hint to understand something by that? Why did You not bless the light too, which You called day, or the firmament of heaven, or the lights, or the stars, or the earth, or the sea? I might say that You, God, who created us in Your image — I might say that it had been Your good pleasure to give this blessing to man alone, if You had not blessed the fishes and the whales in the same way, so that they should increase and multiply and fill the waters of the sea, and blessed the birds so that they should be multiplied on the earth. I might likewise say that this blessing belonged properly to creatures that are bred of their own kind, if I had found it given to the fruit trees and plants and beasts of the earth. But as it is, it is not said to the plants or the trees or the beasts or the serpents, Increase and multiply — although all these, as much as fishes or birds or men, do increase by generation and continue their kind.

What shall I say then, truth, my light? That it was said idly and without meaning? Far be it, Father of devotion, far be it from a minister of Your word to say so. And if I do not understand what You mean by that phrase, let my betters, that is, those with more understanding than I have, make better use of it, as You, my God, have given each man to understand. But let my confession also be pleasing in Your eyes, in which I confess to You that I believe, Lord, that You did not say this in vain; and I shall not suppress what this passage suggests to me. For it is true; and I cannot see what should stop me from understanding the figurative sayings of Your Bible this way. For I know that a thing understood in one way by the mind can be signified in many ways by bodily expressions, and that a thing signified in one way by bodily expression can be understood in many ways in the mind. Consider the single love of God and of our neighbour: by how many sacraments and countless languages, and in each separate language by how many countless ways of speaking, is it bodily expressed! In this way the offspring of the waters increase and multiply. Consider again, whoever is reading this: what Scripture delivers and the voice pronounces in one way only, In the beginning God created heaven and earth — is it not understood in many ways, not through any cheat of error but by various kinds of true meaning? In this way the offspring of man increase and multiply.

So if we take the natures of the things themselves not allegorically but literally, then the phrase increase and multiply fits everything that comes from seed. But if we take the words as spoken figuratively, which I rather suppose is Scripture's purpose, since it surely does not ascribe this blessing superfluously to the offspring of water creatures and of men alone — then we find multitude belonging to spiritual creatures as well as bodily ones, as in heaven and earth; and to the righteous and the unrighteous, as in light and darkness; and to the holy authors who have been the ministers of the law to us, as in the firmament settled between the waters and the waters; and to the society of people still in the bitterness of unbelief, as in the sea; and to the zeal of holy souls, as in the dry land; and to the works of mercy belonging to this present life, as in the plants bearing seed and the trees bearing fruit; and to the spiritual gifts set out for building up, as in the lights of heaven; and to affections shaped to temperance, as in the living soul. In all these cases we meet multitudes and abundance and increase. But what should increase and multiply in such a way that one thing may be expressed in many ways, and one expression understood in many ways, we do not find, except in signs bodily expressed and things conceived in the mind. By signs bodily pronounced we understand the generations of the waters, made necessary by the depth of the flesh; by things conceived in the mind, human generations, on account of the fruitfulness of reason. And this is why we believe You, Lord, to have said to these kinds, Increase and multiply. For in this blessing I take You to have granted us a power and a faculty both for expressing in several ways what we understand in one, and for understanding in several ways what we read delivered obscurely in one. So the waters of the sea are filled, which are not moved except by different meanings; and so the earth too is filled with human increase, whose dryness shows in its longing, and reason rules over it.

I would also say, Lord my God, what the following Scripture reminds me of; yes, I shall say it and not be afraid. For I shall say the truth, with You inspiring me with what You wanted me to bring out of those words. By no other inspiration than Yours do I believe myself to speak truth, seeing that You are the truth and every man a liar. So whoever speaks a lie speaks out of his own; and so, in order to speak truth, I shall speak out of Yours. You have given us for food every plant bearing seed that is on all the earth, and every tree that has in it the fruit of a tree yielding seed. And not to us alone, but to all the birds of the air and the beasts of the earth and all creeping things — but to the fishes and the great whales You have not given them. Now we said that by these fruits of the earth were signified, and figured in an allegory, the works of mercy provided for the necessities of this life out of the fruitful earth. Such an earth was the devout Onesiphorus, on whose house You had mercy, because he often refreshed Your Paul and was not ashamed of his chain. So did the brothers too, and such fruit did they bear, who out of Macedonia supplied what he lacked. But how he grieved over some trees that did not give him the fruit that was owed to him, where he says, At my first defence nobody stood by me, but everyone deserted me. I pray God it may not be charged against them. For these fruits are owed to those who minister spiritual teaching to us out of their understanding of the divine mysteries; and they are owed to them as men; and owed to them too as the living soul, which offers itself as an example in all self-control; and owed to them too as flying creatures, for their blessings which are multiplied over the earth, because their sound went out into all lands.

But those are fed by these fruits who take delight in them; and those are not delighted by them whose god is their belly. For even in those who yield the fruits, it is not the things yielded that are the fruit but the mind they yield them with. So the man who served God and not his own belly — I can plainly see why he rejoiced. I see it, and I rejoice with him. He had received from the Philippians what they had sent him by Epaphroditus; and yet I can see why he rejoiced. For what he rejoiced at is what he fed on; for, speaking truly, I rejoiced greatly in the Lord, he says, that now at last your care for me has flowered again, in which you were careful before, but it had become wearisome to you. So these Philippians had dried up with a long weariness and had withered, as it were, as to bearing this fruit of a good work; and he rejoices for them, that they have flowered again — not for himself, that they had supplied his needs. So he adds, Not that I speak from want; for I have learned, in whatever state I am, to be content with it. I know how to be brought low, and I know how to have plenty; everywhere and in everything I am instructed both to be full and to be hungry, both to have plenty and to go without. I can do all things through him who strengthens me.

What is it, then, that you are rejoicing at, great Paul? What are you rejoicing at? What are you feeding on, man renewed in the knowledge of God after the image of him who created you, living soul of so much self-control, tongue like the flying birds, speaking mysteries — for it is to such creatures that this food is owed? What is it that feeds you? Joy. Let us hear what follows: Nevertheless you have done well, in that you shared in my affliction. This is what he rejoices at; this is what he feeds on: because they had done well, and not because his difficulty was eased — he who says to You, You have made room for me when I was in distress. For he knew how to have plenty and how to go without, in You who strengthen him. For you Philippians know too, he says, that at the beginning of the gospel, when I left Macedonia, no church shared with me in the matter of giving and receiving, but you alone. For even in Thessalonica you sent once and again for my needs. It is to these good works that he now rejoices they have returned; and he is glad that they have flowered again, as when a fruitful field takes back its green.

Was it for his own needs, because he said, You sent for my needs? Is that what he is rejoicing at? Certainly not. But how do we know? Because he says immediately afterwards, Not that I want a gift, but I want fruit. I have learned from You, my God, to distinguish between a gift and fruit. A gift is the thing itself that a man gives when he supplies us with these necessities: money, food, drink, clothing, shelter, help. But the fruit is the good and right will of the giver. For the good master did not only say, Whoever receives a prophet, but added, in the name of a prophet. And he did not only say, Whoever receives a righteous man, but added, in the name of a righteous man. So the one shall truly receive a prophet's reward and the other a righteous man's. And he does not only say, Whoever gives a cup of cold water to one of my little ones, but adds, in the name of a disciple; and so concludes, Truly I say to you, he shall not lose his reward. The gift is to receive a prophet, to receive a righteous man, to give a cup of cold water to a disciple; but the fruit is to do it in the name of a prophet, in the name of a righteous man, in the name of a disciple. Elijah was fed with fruit by the widow who knew she was feeding a man of God and fed him for that reason; but by the raven he was fed with a gift. And it was not Elijah's inner man that was fed that way but only the outer, which might also have died for want of that food.

So I shall say what is true in Your sight, Lord. When unspiritual men and unbelievers, for whose winning and initiation the introductory sacraments and the mighty workings of miracles are necessary — which we take to be signified by the name of fishes and whales — undertake to refresh Your servant bodily, or help him in some other way with something useful for this present life, and yet do not know why this is to be done or to what end, then they are not feeding these men, and these men are not fed by them; because the one lot do not act out of a holy and right intention, and the other do not rejoice at gifts whose fruit they cannot yet see. For the mind feeds on what it is glad of. And so the fishes and whales do not feed on the food the earth does not bring forth until after it has been separated and divided from the bitterness of the waves of the sea.

And You, God, saw everything You had made, and it was very good. And we too see the same, and everything is very good. Of each kind of Your works, when You had said, "let them be", and they were, You saw of each that it was good. Seven times I have counted it written that You saw that what You had made was good; and this is the eighth, that You saw everything You had made, and it was not only good but very good, being now all together. Separately they were only good; all together they were both good and very good. All beautiful bodies say the same, since a body made of members that are all beautiful is far more beautiful than the same members are by themselves, by whose well-ordered blending the whole is completed — although the members separately are beautiful too.

And I looked closely to find whether it was seven times or eight that You saw Your works were good, when they pleased You; and in Your seeing I found no times at all, by which I might understand how often You saw what You had made. And I said, "Lord, is this Scripture of Yours not true, since You are true, and being the truth have put it forth? Then why do You tell me that in Your seeing there are no times, when this Scripture of Yours tells me that what You made each day You saw to be good — and when I counted them, I found how many times?" And to this You answer me, for You are my God, and with a strong voice You tell Your servant in his inner ear, breaking through my deafness and crying out: "Man, what my Scripture says, I say. And yet Scripture speaks in time, while time has no relation to my Word, because my Word exists in an eternity equal to my own. So the things you see through my Spirit, I see; just as what you speak by my Spirit, I speak. And so when you see those things in time, I do not see them in time; just as when you speak in time, I do not speak them in time."

And I heard, Lord my God, and drank a drop of sweetness out of Your truth, and understood that there are certain men who dislike Your works and say that You made many of them under compulsion of necessity, such as the fabric of the heavens and the harmony of the stars; and that You made them not out of what was Yours, but that they were created elsewhere and from other sources, for You to bring together and compact and combine, when out of Your conquered enemies You raised the walls of the universe, so that they, tied down by the structure, should not be able to rebel against You again. And as for other things, they say You neither made them nor even put them together — all flesh, and all the very small creatures, and everything that has its root in the earth; but that a mind at enmity with You, and another nature not created by You and contrary to You, produced and framed these things in the lower stages of the world. They are deranged who say this, because they do not see Your works by Your Spirit, and do not recognise You in them.

But those who see these things by Your Spirit, You see in them. So when they see that these things are good, it is You who see that they are good; and whatever pleases for Your sake, it is You who please in it; and whatever pleases us through Your Spirit, pleases You in us. For what man knows the things of a man, except the spirit of a man which is in him? Even so nobody knows the things of God except the Spirit of God. Now we, he says, have received not the spirit of this world but the Spirit which is from God, so that we may know the things freely given us by God. And I am reminded: "Truly nobody knows the things of God except the Spirit of God; then how do we too know what things are given us by God?" The answer given me is: "Because the things we know by his Spirit, even these nobody knows but the Spirit of God. For as it was rightly said to those who were to speak by the Spirit of God, It is not you who speak, so it is rightly said to those who know through the Spirit of God, It is not you who know. And no less rightly is it said to those who see through the Spirit of God, It is not you who see; so that whatever they see through the Spirit of God to be good, it is not they but God who sees that it is good."

So it is one thing for a man to think something bad which is good, as the men I mentioned do; another for a man to see that something good is good, as Your creatures please many people because they are good, in whom nevertheless You are not what pleases, since they would rather enjoy the creatures than You; and another again for a man, when he sees that a thing is good, for God to see in him that it is good — so that he may be loved in what he has made, who cannot be loved except by the Holy Spirit he has given. Because the love of God is poured into our hearts by the Holy Spirit who is given to us; and it is by him that we see that whatever exists in any degree is good. For it comes from him who is not in any degree, but who is what he is.

Thanks be to You, Lord. We look at heaven and earth, whether the bodily part, higher and lower, or the spiritual and bodily creation. And in the adorning of these parts, which the whole mass of the world, or rather the whole creation, consists of, we see light made and divided from the darkness. We see the firmament of heaven, whether that primary body of the world between the spiritual waters above and the bodily waters below, or — since this too is called heaven — this space of air through which the birds of heaven wander, between those waters that are carried above them in vapour and drip down as dew on clear nights, and the heavier waters that flow along the earth. We look at the face of the waters gathered together in the fields of the sea; and the dry land, both empty and then formed so as to be visible and ordered, and the material of plants and trees. We look at the lights shining from above: the sun to suffice for the day, and the moon and stars to cheer the night; and by all of these times are to be marked and signified. We see on all sides a moist element, filled with fishes and beasts and birds, because the thickness of the air which holds up the flights of birds is thickened by the exhalation of the waters. We look at the face of the earth decked with earthly creatures, and man, created after Your image and likeness, set over all irrational creatures by that very image and likeness of Yours, which is the power of reason and understanding. And as in his soul there is one power that has command by directing, and another made subject so that it should obey, so also for the man there was made, bodily, a woman, who in the mind of her reasoning understanding should have an equality of nature, but in the sex of her body should be subject in the same way to the sex of her husband, as the appetite for doing is glad to take the skill of right-doing from the reason of the mind. These things we look at, and separately they are good, and all together very good.

Let Your works praise You, so that we may love You; and let us love You, so that Your works may praise You — works that from time have a beginning and an end, a rising and a setting, growth and decay, form and loss of form. So they have their succession of morning and evening, partly in secret and partly in the open; for they were made out of nothing, by You, not out of You; not out of any matter that was not Yours or that existed before, but out of matter created along with them — that is, created by You at the same time — because You gave form to its formless state without any interval of time. For seeing that the matter of heaven and earth is one thing and the form another, You made the matter out of nothing at all, but the form of the world out of the matter without form; and yet both together, so that the form followed the matter with no interval of delay.

We have also examined what You wanted foreshadowed, whether by the creation or by the telling of things in such an order. And we have seen that things separately are good and together very good — in Your Word, in Your only-begotten: both heaven and earth, the head and the body of the church, in Your predestination before all times, with no morning and evening. But when You began to carry out in time the things predestined, so that You might reveal hidden things and put our disorders right — for our sins hung over us and we had sunk into the dark deep, and Your good Spirit was borne over us to help us at the right moment; and You made the ungodly righteous and divided them from the wicked; and You made the firmament of the authority of Your book between those placed above, who were to be teachable towards You, and those below, who were to be subject to them. And You gathered the society of unbelievers together into one conspiracy, so that the zeal of the faithful might show, and they might bring forth works of mercy, distributing their earthly riches to the poor so as to gain heavenly ones. And after this You lit certain lights in the firmament, Your holy ones, holding the word of life and shining with an eminent authority set on high through spiritual gifts. And after that again, for the initiation of the unbelieving Gentiles, You produced out of bodily matter the sacraments and visible miracles and forms of words according to the firmament of Your book, by which the faithful should be blessed and multiplied. Next You formed the living soul of the faithful, through affections well ordered by the strength of self-control. And after that, the mind subject to You alone and needing to imitate no human authority You have renewed after Your image and likeness; and You made its rational actions subject to the excellence of the understanding, as the woman is to the man. And for all the offices of Your ministry, necessary for the perfecting of the faithful in this life, You willed that good things should be given by those same faithful for their temporary use, fruitful for the givers in the time to come. All these things we see, and they are very good, because You see them in us — You who have given us Your Spirit, by which we might see them, and in them love You.

Lord God, give us peace, since You have given us everything: the peace of rest, the peace of the Sabbath, which has no evening. For all this most beautiful array of things, very good as it is, must pass away when it has finished its course; for in them there was morning and evening.

But the seventh day has no evening and no setting, because You have made it holy to last for ever. So that what You did after Your works, which were very good, resting on the seventh day — although You made them in unbroken rest — may be announced to us beforehand by the voice of Your book: that we too, after our works, which are very good precisely because You have given them to us, shall rest in You as well, in the Sabbath of eternal life.

For then You will rest in us, as now You work in us; and so that will be Your rest through us, as these are Your works through us. But You, Lord, are always working and always at rest. You do not see in time, or move in time, or rest in a time; and yet You make the things that are seen in time, and the times themselves, and the rest that results from time.

So we see these things You have made, because they exist; but they exist because You see them. And we see outwardly that they exist, and inwardly that they are good; but You saw them there, once made, where You had seen them as still to be made. And we were moved at a later time to do good, after our hearts had conceived of Your Spirit; but in the earlier time we were moved to do evil, abandoning You. But You, the one good God, never stopped doing good. And we too have some good works, by Your gift, but not eternal ones; and after them we trust to rest in Your great hallowing. But You, being the good which needs no good, are always at rest, because Your rest is Yourself. And what man can teach a man to understand this? Or what angel, an angel? Or what angel, a man? Let it be asked of You, sought in You, knocked for at You. So, so shall it be received; so shall it be found; so shall it be opened. Amen.

	Gratias tibi, Domine — thanks be to You, Lord.
